- Duplicate accounts, such as one made with a personal email and one with a business email, often cause audiences and product feeds to be missing. Check that each linked account number matches the intended account. [L1204.055]
- Google Analytics shows conversion rate for each product page in its dashboard. This is hard to see directly in Shopify when running ads for four or five products. [L1213.069]
- Duplicate tracking: if a Google dynamic remarketing tag already exists on the site, remove it before using the app. Keeping both counts every sale twice. [L1165.019]
- A "no conversions" warning is not proof of a fault if the tag was added recently and no conversion has been recorded yet. [L1115.103]
- If the GA4 test sales do not appear, check the GA4 connection through the Google & YouTube app. [L1287.063]

## Setting up Meta, TikTok, Pinterest and other pixels

### Pixel basics and how Meta uses them

- A Facebook pixel is code on the website that records events such as page views, add to carts and purchases. The data goes back to Meta, where the algorithm uses it to optimise delivery and find similar buyers, and it also shows sales in Ads Manager. [L1024.020]
- Standard Meta conversion events for an online store are Add to Cart (ATC), Initiate Checkout (IC) and Purchase. They mark the steps a visitor takes toward buying, and Meta uses them to measure performance. [L1021.008]
- The pixel only records events that have already happened on the site. At launch it contributes nothing, so early results depend on creative and initial targeting. [L0639.010]
- After purchases, the pixel learns who bought and searches for similar users, so early sales from a pocket improve later delivery. A competitor with more pixel data can outperform an identical setup early on. [L0639.011]
- In Events Manager, check which events the pixel tracks: website visits, add to cart, initiate checkout and purchases. [L0645.034]
- The pixel appears as an audience source. Select it and confirm it shows green, which means it is installed and active. [L1023.005]
- Purchase events sent from the store to Meta put buyers into the purchase audiences. [L1023.005]
- Facebook retargeting runs on the Facebook pixel. Once the pixel is installed in the store, the ad setup loads it automatically, so no separate tracking setup is needed in the ad. (Older advice, 2021-2022.) [L1342.039]
- Before you judge results such as ROAS or purchases, confirm the Meta pixel is connected to the Shopify store through the Facebook and Instagram channel app. Sales only reach Ads Manager through that connection, so without it the reported numbers are unreliable. [L1024.046]

### Pixel installation methods

- A tracking pixel is a small script on every page that records visitor activity and actions. Google calls the same thing a tag. All major ad networks use this cookie-based system to fill retargeting lists. (Older advice, 2021-2022.) [L1390.005]
- A pixel can be installed by placing the code directly in the site or through Google Tag Manager. Google Tag Manager is not mandatory. It suits extra tags such as Twitter or Taboola. (Older advice, 2021-2022.) [L1390.009]

### Shopify-Meta connection, data sharing and Conversions API

- Since Apple's iOS 14.5 update, data sharing from third-party sites such as Shopify to Facebook is heavily restricted on Apple devices. Maximum data sharing is used to recover part of that tracking. [L1318.005]
- In the Facebook shop setup:
  1. Switch on data sharing.
  2. Choose one of three levels: Standard, Enhanced or Maximum.
  3. Select Maximum. [L1318.003]
- Turn on Automatic Advanced Matching during the connection summary before you continue, because the tracking setup needs it. [L1318.004]
- Maximum data sharing combines all data sharing options and uses the Conversions API (CAPI), which sends data directly from Shopify servers to Facebook. Because this path is server-side, ad blockers cannot block the data. [L1318.006]
- Shopify's native Facebook/Meta integration with maximum data sharing enables CAPI alongside the pixel. CAPI sends extra data with each conversion: IP address, email, name and phone number. Shopify reportedly attributes 90% or more of conversions correctly with this setup. [L0666.009]
- Enable Shopify's Facebook/Meta integration with maximum data sharing to activate CAPI alongside the pixel. The pixel alone is the least reliable option because it is the most blocked or anonymised. [L0666.010]

### Domain verification and Facebook setup order

Source C procedure:
1. Verify your domain in Meta, so that data Shopify sends back is recognised as coming from a domain you own. [L1158.039]
2. Connect the Meta pixel through the Shopify Meta app. [L1158.039]
3. In Events Manager, set up the top aggregated events in this priority: Purchase, Initiate Checkout, Add to Cart, Search, View Content. [L1158.039, L1158.043]
4. Add UTM parameters at ad level (campaign name, ad set name, ad name) to every ad URL, so Shopify can record which ad each sale came from. [L1158.039]

The sources disagree on the number of events. One says the top **8** aggregated events [L1158.039], the other says **5** [L1158.043]. Both give the same five events in the same order. The units name only these five, so the other three are not specified here.

### Per-product pixels and pixel strategy

| Situation | Action | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Testing a new product | Create a separate pixel for each product you test | [L1193.001] |
| New product, same niche, pixel already has buyer data | Keep the same pixel | [L1476.046] |
| Several products in the same niche, one email list | Share one pixel and one list across launches | [L0307.036] |
| Niche changes | Change the pixel | [L1476.046] |
| Changing the pixel after every test | Another practitioner advised this; a personal choice | [L1476.047] |

- Sharing one pixel across several product tests combines their conversions, so you cannot tell which product sold. [L1193.007]
- Keep one central email list of all previous customers and leads. Each new launch uses the same warm audience and pixel data, so campaigns can be profitable immediately without weeks of pixel warm-up. Example: a supplement brand with a shared pixel and a 180K email list generated $250K revenue from $9K ad spend across 4 product launches. [L0307.036]
- A pixel with buying data from the same niche gives Facebook more to work with, which can lower CPMs and make targeting more accurate. [L1476.047]
- Old pixels can be deleted to keep the account clean. [L1476.046]

#### Creating a test pixel for one product

1. In Shopify, search for the Meta app and open it.
2. Go to the app's Settings page.
3. Click Create new to make a new pixel.
4. Check that the new pixel appears at the top of the list and double-check its ID.
5. Assign people to manage the pixel and give them access to all your profiles.
6. Assign assets: select all your ad accounts.
7. Open Events Manager from the assets view.
8. Rename the pixel to today's date plus the product name.
9. Close Events Manager, refresh the Shopify page, and check that the same pixel is shown. [L1193.002]

- Name each test pixel with today's date plus the product name, in the format DDMM plus product name, for example 2404 SleepMask, so you can match it to its test later. [L1193.003]
- Meta has moved pixel data assets. To find a Meta Pixel, go to Data Sources and select Datasets, not the older location. [L1193.004]
- After creating a pixel, finish installing it on the store, including adding events, before expecting tracking to start. The Events Manager overview shows whether the installation is still open. [L1193.005]
- Website events no longer need a domain selected in the ad account. The website events dataset connected to the ad account is tracked by default. URL parameters stay optional. [L1193.006]

### Lead-generation pixel

- For a lead pixel, create a pixel with the Leads conversion event and give it a name. Paste the generated code into the head section of the thank-you page, where people land after signing up on the squeeze page. Do not place the pixel on the squeeze page itself. [L0641.009]

### TikTok pixel and optimisation

- TikTok's native tracking is unreliable and tracks approximately 50% of actual sales. Use tracking apps or UTM parameters for accurate performance measurement. [L0298.042]
- Website conversions require a TikTok pixel. Install it on the Shopify store through the TikTok pixel integration, then log in through TikTok Ads Manager so the pixel connects automatically. You never need to link your TikTok account to get the pixel. [L1383.008]
- Set the optimisation event to Complete Payment, not Add to Cart. Add to Cart optimisation reports cheap add-to-cart conversions that look like sales but are not, which misleads you about profitability. Complete Payment usually costs slightly more per result. [L1383.009]
- Use Complete Payment as the optimisation event, so TikTok optimises toward completed purchases rather than cheaper actions. [L1378.004]
- Create multiple pixels and do not connect them to the store. TikTok treats an ad account as new when its pixel shows no recent activity, which can lead to stronger results. [L1378.005]
- Unlike Facebook, where pixel history and data volume matter most, TikTok handles pixels differently, so creating a new pixel from time to time is recommended. [L1378.006]

The sources conflict on connection. One says to install the pixel through the store integration for website conversions [L1383.008]. Another says to create extra pixels and not connect them to the store [L1378.005]. The units do not explain how the two fit together.

- Do not run Facebook and TikTok ads for the same product at the same time. The traffic behaves differently and mixes the data in the Shopify and Meta pixel. TikTok traffic often adds to cart and reaches checkout without buying, which makes decisions harder. [L1017.016]

### Pinterest conversion tracking

- The Pinterest tag, installed through the Shopify marketing section, is the essential tracking step. It records the visits and purchases that Pinterest uses to optimise ads. [L1135.025]
- Pinterest does not allow conversion campaigns until the account has conversion events. The error appears as a message asking you to increase your conversion events. [L1135.026]

### Tracking apps and integrations

| Tool | Use | Cost or threshold | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| UTM tracking | Starting point for smaller stores | None stated | [L1345.094] |
| Trackify | Sales tracking for smaller stores | None stated | [L1345.094] |
| Zapier | Automations that send sales data back to Facebook | About $20-$29 per month | [L1345.094] |
| Cometly | Tracking data and sending it back to Meta | Recommended once monthly revenue is above about $20K-$30K | [L1345.093] |
| Triple Whale | Option for tracking | About $200-$300 per month | [L1345.093] |
| Tixel | TikTok pixel app | Not stated | [L0298.043] |
| Triple Whale, Converge, Cometly | Other TikTok pixel tracking apps | Most require paid subscriptions | [L0298.043] |
| OptiMonk | Google Analytics integration for popup performance; email notification on each subscription | Not stated | [L1138.014] |
| Shopify Facebook channel app | Shows how many purchases the server sent compared with what the pixel tracked | Not stated | [L0645.034] |

## UTM tracking and attribution

### Attribution principles and their limits

- Digital marketing channels influence each other rather than operating independently. PPC campaigns create lift beyond their direct metrics by affecting traffic patterns and performance across other channels. [L0026.001]
- Perfect tracking is impossible because modern users comparison shop, visit multiple stores, and take time to evaluate purchases, so it is unclear which touchpoint should receive conversion credit. [L0033.001]
- Attribution means crediting conversions to traffic sources. It is always imperfect because customers interact with multiple channels and touchpoints across several days before purchasing. [L0257.001]
- Choose one tracking and attribution model and apply it consistently across all reporting periods instead of shifting the measurement approach. All tracking systems have blind spots (cookie loss, multi-session journeys), so consistency matters more than perfect accuracy. [L0257.014]
- Evaluate campaign performance by comparing total revenue generated against total ad spend, not by relying solely on last-click attribution data. [L0033.004, L1406.003]
- Track revenue by traffic source from the start. Early tracking shows which sources bring profit and which waste time. Test sources, keep those that consistently make money, and drop the rest. [L0597.005]
- Judge each traffic source by the revenue and profit it generates, not by the number of visitors it sends. A source that delivers many visitors but no sales is a cost, not a win. [L0597.015]
- Set up sales tracking before the store gets traffic, so every sale can be traced back to the ad or source that brought it. *(Older: Source A 2020.)* [L0468.168]
- Track sales coming through phone, text or live chat, which Google does not record. [L0470.068]
  1. Ask each customer how they found the store when you take the order.
  2. Optionally add a short survey to the order confirmation page, such as a simple form asking how the customer found you.
  3. Note answers such as Google search, Bing search or a Facebook ad.

  [L0470.068]
- Platform tracking for Facebook, Google and email is often incomplete, partly because of iOS 14 limits and changed tracking rules from around 2022 on. Ask buyers how they found the store in a post-purchase survey and compare those answers with platform revenue before trusting either number alone. [L1204.068]

### Multi-session journeys and double counting

- The majority of e-commerce sites generate more revenue from multi-session customers (returning visitors) than from first-time single-session purchases. [L0257.003]
- Realistic journey over several days and channels: Monday the customer clicks a shopping campaign; Tuesday they click a search retargeting ad and become a lead; Wednesday they convert via email; Thursday they share a referral code. A friend sees the site, clicks YouTube retargeting, and converts. The result is 2 actual purchases but 4 to 5 channels claiming credit. [L0257.007]
- When multiple platforms independently track the same conversion (email, YouTube, shopping, referral software), each counts it separately. This produces 4 to 5 counted conversions when only 1 or 2 actual purchases occurred. [L0257.008]
- Customer journeys can span many days and several touchpoints, so the time from first ad to purchase is often long. In one example, one stage took 5 days and 2 hours and the next took 2 days and 22 hours. Customers sometimes take 7 to 10 or more days to buy. [L0688.014]
- Typical multi-touch paths include seeing a Facebook or Instagram ad, later receiving an email or SMS, returning through a retargeting ad, visiting via Google search, or completing an abandoned cart email flow. Judge attribution across the whole path, not only the last click. [L0688.015]
- Shoppers often need several visits or ad clicks before buying, so many conversions are multi-step and only the last touch gets credit in simple reports. A high share of multi-step conversions is expected and does not indicate a broken setup. Single-click reports will consistently undercount earlier touchpoints. [L1406.015]

### Direct and unattributed traffic

- Direct/None traffic represents customers returning through browser history or text message referrals. In mature stores this channel shows 15 percent of attributed revenue but is largely unmeasurable as a true traffic source. [L0257.009]
- Direct/None traffic consists of visits with no recorded referral source, for example returning visitors opening the site from their history, clicks on untracked links, or links shared in private messages. In the example store it was the third largest traffic source and produced 12 to 13 percent of total revenue. [L1406.007]
- Direct traffic in analytics can come from untracked links, bookmarks, or links saved from email, and it may be large. Keep it separate from paid ad traffic when reading results. *(Older: Source A 2020.)* [L0967.086]
- Do not treat Direct/None as a source you can scale or optimize directly. It hides untracked touchpoints, so use it only for comparison between channels. [L1406.008]
- Direct/None can account for a large share of total sales in a Shopify store. Channel-level revenue from Google Analytics therefore will not add up to the store's total revenue. Use it to compare channels relative to each other, not to reconcile totals. [L1406.016]
- Direct/None traffic in Analytics typically represents the final purchase session, where Google cannot link the purchase to earlier discovery visits. Reallocate this revenue to the sources that actually brought the customer (usually organic or paid search). *(Older: Source B 2021-22.)* [L0256.011]
- Estimating the share of unattributed revenue by source (*Older: Source B 2021-22*):
  - When Shopify records revenue that Analytics does not attribute to a traffic source, estimate each source's portion by calculating its share of new users and applying the same percentage to unattributed revenue. [L0256.009]
  - Example: if Google Ads drove 72.9% of new users, attribute 72.9% of the untracked revenue to Google Ads. [L0256.009]

### Abandoned cart, email and message-driven sales

- UTMs track the source of email-driven sales by appearing as parameters at the end of URLs, enabling reporting in Shopify Messaging and Google Analytics. [L0147.010]
- Abandoned cart recovery emails originate from an earlier visit through another traffic source. Attribute these sales to the source that first brought the customer, not to the email channel. *(Older: Source B 2021-22.)* [L0256.010]
- Multi-touch attribution tools can pull email and SMS touchpoints from platforms such as Klaviyo and Attentive into the customer journey. This links a sale to an earlier ad even when the final touch before purchase was a message. [L0688.017]

### Reallocating revenue and cross-channel effects

- Evaluating each channel only by its own direct clicks and sales misses cross-channel effects, such as a YouTube campaign lifting search click-through rates or total traffic. Cutting a channel that looks weak in isolation can reduce results in the channels that depend on it. [L1142.010]
- When a new Google Ads search campaign goes live, total site traffic may rise gradually over several weeks rather than immediately. Judge cross-channel lift by watching the overall traffic trend over that period, not only the clicks reported for each campaign. [L1142.011]
- To compare paid Google Ads traffic with organic search and email, use the channel comparison in Google Analytics. To find which individual ads convert and how often, use Google Ads data. Do not mix the two views when judging a single campaign. [L1406.017]

### Attribution models and windows

- Google Ads uses a 30-day lookback window by default, attributing calls to website visits within the previous 30 days. [L0234.007]
- Last-click attribution is the standard model for call conversions in Google Ads, giving credit to the final ad or source a person clicked before calling. [L0234.008]
- Meta default attribution for this setup credits a purchase to the ad if the person clicked it within the last 7 days or viewed it within the last 1 day. Keep these defaults for remarketing campaigns. [L1029.011]
- The click window is kept long because a person who clicked a remarketing ad days earlier and bought later was still reached by that ad. A view counts because the ad reminds the person of the store even without a click. [L1029.012]
- Use a 30-day attribution window for influencer marketing rather than a one-day click window. Influencers typically need multiple exposures, often three or more touches, before a customer buys. [L0790.075]
- Retargeting apps often claim revenue using a 30-day view attribution window, which credits them with sales they did not generate. Check app attribution against your own analytics before trusting it. [L0790.079]
- Account hygiene: use 7DC / 1DEV attribution, and exclude website purchasers from the last 180 days and existing Shopify customers from targeting. [L1019.045]

Attribution settings compared against AMER (Acquisition Marketing Efficiency Ratio):

- AMER measures how efficiently the whole business acquires new customers, so it is the business-level truth against which attribution settings are judged. Ad platform numbers alone should not decide which setting is better. [L1022.007]
- If an account is optimized toward a metric that does not correlate with AMER, it is not optimizing for the real business outcome. Change the attribution setting or optimization target to one that correlates with AMER. [L1022.010]
- Platform-wide changes by Meta cause a temporary drop in AMER across the whole industry. Performance returns to normal only gradually and not fully to the previous level. Expect a dip after any platform change and do not read it as a permanent failure of the account. [L1022.011]

| Setting | What the sources say | Use or caution | Ids |
|---|---|---|---|
| 7-day click | Before March 17 it had the highest correlation with AMER among the tested settings, which is why it was the default. Gives roughly seven times the window of 1-day click. | Default before the change. | [L1022.008] |
| 1-day click | Too narrow for most accounts because it did not capture enough purchases for fast decisions. | Caution. | [L1022.008] |
| 1-day click plus 1-day view | Showed a higher correlation in the chart. | Its window is too short for most accounts. | [L1022.009] |
| 7-day click plus 1-day engage-through | After the redefinition, matched AMER better than the other tested settings. | Seven-figure cohort, March 2026. | [L1022.009] |
| 7DC / 1DEV | Recommended account hygiene setting. | Use. | [L1019.045] |

- A cohort of seven-figure stores tracked in March 2026 showed AMER dropping sharply at the March 18 attribution redefinition, then recovering only partly over the following weeks. Before the change, 7-day click followed AMER closely; afterwards it diverged. [L1022.009]

### UTM parameters: what they do and naming

- UTM parameters are free extra fields appended to the destination URL of an ad. When a visitor clicks, they pass source, medium, campaign, content and term to analytics tools such as Shopify reports or Google Analytics. [L0688.003]
- UTM tracking shows which campaign, ad set and ad a sale came from. Compare these sources in Shopify reports or in Google Analytics if it is connected to Shopify. [L0648.029]
- UTM tracking is limited because customers often buy on a later visit, from another device, or after several touchpoints such as ads, emails, SMS and Google searches. Those paths can break the link to the original ad, so UTM data loses sales. [L0688.008]
- Use UTMs to cross-check Facebook metrics against actual store data. [L0691.025]
- Use a consistent naming convention: name ad sets by date, audience, country, placement, and ads after their video or angle. The names flow into the URL parameters, which keeps results readable. [L0647.014]
- Use UTM parameters on every TikTok ad URL. Name campaigns, ad groups and ads consistently so the parameters can be reconciled with Shopify orders. [L1377.034]
- Track every ad, headline and offer with its own key, such as a unique coupon, code, address or phone number, so each response can be traced to its source. Without keying, the best-performing creative cannot be identified. [L1261.016]
- UTM parameters show conversion rate per campaign and ad set, which is more precise than country-level data for budget decisions. Check UTM tracking when deciding which ad set or campaign deserves more money within a country. [L1316.009]
- Some sellers do not connect the Meta pixel to their store and instead track each campaign with UTM parameters, reading which campaign converts from the UTM data. This allows testing across several ad accounts without linking the pixel to each one. [L0638.056]
- Small brands rarely need custom parameters. Bigger brands and GA4 or Triple Whale users should follow the parameters their tool requires. [L0865.052]
- Audience segment URL parameters are set for all sales campaigns in the advertising settings, not per ad, so they do not need to change for each new ad. [L0865.053]
- UTM Link Builder creates tracking URLs with utm_source, utm_medium and utm_campaign parameters. These let analytics attribute visits from ads, emails and links to their source. [L1007.005]

### UTM strings and builder procedures by platform

Shared Meta/Facebook ad-level string (L0375.008 and L0376.011 are the same string, merged):

```
utm_source=Facebook&utm_medium=cpc&utm_campaign={{campaign.name}}&utm_content={{ad.name}}&utm_term={{adset.name}}
```

[L0375.008, L0376.011]

- Add UTM parameters at the ad level so traffic from each ad is tracked by campaign, ad and ad set. Use exactly this parameter string. [L0376.010]
- Note: the sources give different conventions for the same fields. The string above maps utm_content to the ad and utm_term to the ad set. Procedure L0648.028 sets utm term to the ad set name, and procedure L0688.004 sets utm content to the ad set name and utm term to the ad name. Choose one convention and apply it consistently (see L0257.014). [L0375.008, L0376.010, L0648.028, L0688.004]

Meta procedure in the ad's website section (Ads Manager):

1. Click Build a URL parameter.
2. Set campaign source to facebook.
3. Set campaign medium to cpc (cost per click, the standard label for paid clicks).
4. Set campaign name to the dynamic value campaign.name from the dropdown.
5. Add the ad set name and the ad name as dynamic values from the dropdown.
6. Set utm term to the ad set name.

The parameters are added to the URL, so Shopify receives which campaign, ad set and ad sent the visitor. [L0648.028]

Free UTM setup for a Facebook ad (procedure):

1. In Facebook Ads Manager, open the ad level of the campaign after adding the page, creative, and website URL (the landing page or product page).
2. Open the section called Tracking.
3. In the UTM parameters area, either use a pre-built UTM link from a website or use Facebook's own UTM builder.
4. Fill in Campaign source: Facebook, FB Marketing, or any consistent label you prefer.
5. Fill in Campaign medium: CPC (cost per click) or the placement information.
6. Fill in Campaign name with the campaign name.
7. Fill in Campaign content with the ad set name.
8. Fill in UTM term with the ad name.
9. Save the URL so the parameters are appended when someone clicks Shop Now or any other button.

[L0688.004]

Meta setting in Source E's course:

- Ad > Tracking > URL parameters > Build a URL parameter: add utm_source with the value ((campaign.id)) or the campaign name. Add utm_medium for the medium, such as Facebook Feed, utm_campaign for the campaign or promotion, and utm_content for the ad variant. Dynamic values like ((ad.id)) can be inserted from the campaign, ad set or ad level. [L0865.051]

Checking UTM results in Shopify:

- Go to Reports and search for "sales through marketing" or sessions attributed to marketing. Select the exact time range, for example today, then add or view the columns UTM term, source, campaign name, campaign medium, and content. The columns stay empty until traffic arrives with UTM parameters. [L0688.005]
- Shopify's sales-through-marketing report, together with Google Analytics, shows which source, medium, campaign, content and term brought each session or sale when UTM links are used. It is the free way to see campaign-level attribution without an extra subscription. [L0688.006]

Meta ad-level tracking with UTMs (on iOS and beyond):

- After the iOS privacy changes, Facebook and Shopify struggle to track what happens after the click. The destination link should carry campaign, ad set and ad names as URL parameters, so Shopify can show which ad set and ad each customer came from. [L0647.013]
- Accurate attribution is needed to decide whether to kill, keep or scale a campaign or ad set. Without knowing which campaign or ad set produced each sale, a profitable account cannot be scaled with confidence. [L0688.002]

### TikTok and Pinterest UTM setup

TikTok:

- UTM links work for TikTok ads too. Paste a UTM link into the store link field at the ad level instead of a plain store URL, using the same parameter logic as for Facebook. [L0688.007]
- On TikTok, UTM parameters are needed because native TikTok tracking was considered unreliable. Compare Google Analytics with TikTok ad data and duplicate only the campaigns that perform well. *(Date not stated in source.)* [L0819.019]
- Use UTM parameters on every TikTok ad URL, because tracking on iOS is limited and TikTok data may not match Shopify. [L1377.034]

Pinterest string appended to the destination URL:

```
?utm_source=pinterest&utm_medium=cpc&utm_campaign=%7Bcampaignname%7D&utm_term=%7Badgroupname%7D&utm_content=%7Badid%7D
```

[L1133.008]

- Destination URL: use the product page link and append UTM parameters with a question mark to track which campaign the sales come from. [L1134.029]
- Destination URL: enter the product page link, then append a UTM tag starting with a question mark so each sale can be traced to its campaign, ad set and creative. [L1135.045]
- Pinterest tracking is delayed because the platform takes time to record sales. UTM parameters show the source of each sale and are sufficient for a simple one campaign, one ad group test. [L1135.046]

### iOS and platform undercounting

- Since Apple's iOS 14 update in 2021, Facebook Ads Manager reports on campaign and ad set sales have become less reliable, especially for Apple devices. Many accounts see tracking accuracy of roughly 60 to 80 percent, so decisions should not rely on Ads Manager data alone. [L0688.001]
- Since iOS 14, Facebook reporting undercounts conversions. The coach estimated that only about 70 percent of actual purchases may show up there. The platform dashboard can therefore show a return of 1.3 when the real value is closer to 1.6. Use an independent tracking source to check the numbers. [L0694.007]
- Facebook misses some sales when users switch devices or browsers, or when cookies are not stored. Complex tools such as Triple Whale can track the full customer journey, but they are not needed for the basic 80 to 90 percent tracking rate. [L1158.041]
- After iOS tracking changes, Meta can track fewer users, so its algorithm is less precise. Retargeting still works but needs different setups. Advertisers have moved toward broad audiences with Advantage+ campaigns that receive all creatives and handle retargeting internally. [L0353.026]
- Tracking accuracy figures differ by measurement: 60 to 80 percent is the accuracy many accounts see in Ads Manager (L0688.001); about 70 percent is the coach's estimate of purchases that show up (L0694.007); 80 to 90 percent is the basic tracking rate that does not need a complex tool (L1158.041).
- UTM tracking is not 100 percent accurate after the iOS update, but it still shows which campaign, ad set and ad a sale came from. [L0648.029]

### Paid multi-touch attribution tools

Tools and what they do:

- Paid multi-touch attribution tools include Triple Whale, Northbeam, Cometly, Converge and Trackify. They track customer journeys across Facebook, TikTok, Google, email and SMS. Triple Whale also shows platform ROAS next to its own ROAS. [L0688.009]
- Triple Whale's dashboard lists campaigns in a layout similar to Facebook Ads Manager, with spend and ROAS per campaign. Platform-reported ROAS and the tool's ROAS can be compared side by side on each row, making any gap easy to see. [L0688.016]
- Triple Whale is better for attribution, and its AI on a higher paid plan is strong. Use TrueProfit when the main goal is profit tracking and Triple Whale when the main goal is attribution. [L1299.028]
- Google Analytics custom report setup is free, and paid attribution tools are generally expensive. Google Analytics with a properly installed tag remains the standard for high-ticket dropshipping in most cases. *(Older: Source B 2021-22.)* [L0721.032]

Cost and threshold:

| Item | Value | Note | Ids |
|---|---|---|---|
| Starting price, Triple Whale, Northbeam, Cometly, Converge | About 100 to 150 USD per month | Rises with revenue or monthly visitors | [L0688.011] |
| Trackify | Cheaper | Weaker interface; mainly sends conversion data back to Facebook | [L0688.011] |
| Worth-it threshold (monthly revenue) | Roughly 20,000 USD or more | Below that, UTM parameters are recommended | [L0688.010] |
| Worth-it threshold (monthly ad spend) | Roughly 10,000 USD or more | Older, Source B 2021-22. Installing a more advanced tool only makes economic sense at this level, because such tools are expensive. | [L0721.021] |

- Attribution is not a perfect science. Installing a more advanced attribution tool only makes economic sense at roughly 10,000 USD or more per month in ad spend, because such tools are expensive. *(Older: Source B 2021-22.)* [L0721.021]
- Paid attribution tools are only worth their cost at roughly 20,000 USD or more in monthly revenue. Below that level, UTM parameters are recommended. [L0688.010]

Example of platform versus tool ROAS:

- A Facebook campaign with 244 USD spend showed a ROAS of 0.84 in Facebook Ads Manager, while Triple Whale showed 1.30 for the same campaign. Another campaign showed 1.22 on Facebook and 2.75 in Triple Whale. A campaign that looks unprofitable on the platform can be profitable once tracking is corrected, which changes the kill-or-scale decision. [L0688.012]
- Simple case: an account spends 200 USD on Facebook ads and earns 400 USD in sales, so it is profitable. Without campaign or ad set level tracking, the owner cannot tell which ad set generated the sales and so cannot decide which one to scale. [L0688.013]

### Platform versus analytics reconciliation

Three data layers and their jobs:

- Three data layers answer three different questions. The store platform or accounting software (e.g. Shopify, Xero, QuickBooks) shows total ad spend against total revenue. Google Ads shows campaign-level return on ad spend (ROAS). Google Analytics compares traffic channels with each other. Each layer has its own job and should not be used for the others. [L1406.001]
- Use the accounting or store platform to judge overall advertising efficiency, meaning total ad spend compared with total revenue. [L1406.003]
- Use Google Analytics to compare traffic sources with each other, and use Google Ads to optimize Google Ads campaigns. Treat the revenue number of a single source as a comparison figure, not as its exact true value. [L1406.006]

Worked gap example:

- In the example store, for the same period, Google Analytics reported 376,593 USD in revenue and Google Ads reported 552,255 USD. The gap was about 175,000 USD, or almost 46 percent of the Analytics figure. A difference like this is normal because each tool tracks conversions differently. [L1406.004]
- When reconciling revenue between Google Analytics and Google Ads, compare the same store over the exact same date range. Different periods alone can create a large gap before any tracking difference is considered. [L1406.014]
- A shopper may click one ad on one day and buy days later through another ad or channel, and the earlier interaction often is not recorded correctly. This missing credit is the most likely reason for differences between platforms in active campaigns. When comparing data levels, always consider assisted conversions first. [L1406.005]

Multi-Channel Funnels and conversion paths:

- Google Analytics Multi-Channel Funnels and attribution reports show the different sequences of touchpoints that lead to a conversion. They try to close some data gaps, but information is still lost. [L1406.009]
- In the 30-day example, the store generated 1,670,000 USD in revenue and 361 conversions. In the Multi-Channel Funnels view used, 209 of these conversions (about 60 percent) were multi-step, multi-click conversions. The Multi-Channel Funnels report only showed conversions with more than two steps. [L1406.010]
- The speaker estimated, without data to back it up, that the true share of multi-click conversions in that store was probably closer to 70 percent. Treat this as an unverified guess. [L1406.011]
- Conversion paths labeled "unavailable" mean the data is missing. In the example, "unavailable" was the top conversion source and the seventh, appearing 142 times in the list. Do not read these entries as a real traffic channel. [L1406.012]
- To count "unavailable" entries in the Multi-Channel Funnels conversion path list, set the list to show 250 rows and search for "unavailable". The number of matches shows how much data is missing in that list. [L1406.013]
- Google Analytics is typically more accurate than Google Ads for conversion reporting because it uses a different tracking mechanism and better recognizes when a single purchase results from multiple earlier visits. *(Older: Source B 2021-22.)* [L0256.006]

Google Ads revenue and Analytics choice:

- Google & YouTube analytics integration attributes sales to their source, such as Meta, organic Google, alpha campaign or beta campaign. Use these attributions to judge whether each channel is profitable. [L0972.055]
- Google Analytics compares traffic channels; Google Ads reports campaign ROAS. Use the two only for their own job, as set out above. [L1406.001, L1406.006]

Meta reporting checks:

- Meta's Purchase ROAS = attributed purchase revenue divided by amount spent. Meta's value is approximate because it is based on purchases attributed to the ads, including sales through Shops, Marketplace, Pages or Messenger, and some values may be estimated by third-party technologies. [L1030.004]
- Meta shows a notice about changed attribution reporting for clicks and engagement results. Check the "About this update" notice before comparing results across time periods. [L1030.006]
- Default Meta ad-set attribution and reconciliation: use the account's own store data as the reference, as set out in the iOS and tool sections above. [L0694.007, L0688.001]

### Offline sales, phone calls and keyed responses

Google Ads call tracking:

- Implement call tracking in Google Ads when you receive more than 30 calls per month to understand their source and performance. [L0234.001]
- Calls shorter than 20 seconds do not count as conversions. This filters out very brief or incomplete calls. [L0234.006]
- Each website visitor is assigned a unique tracking phone number. When the same person returns to the site, they see the same number, which allows their actions to be attributed to their original traffic source. [L0234.002]
- Google displays a forwarding number to each visitor in place of your actual phone number. When a visitor dials the forwarding number, Google immediately routes the call to your real number and records it as a conversion. [L0234.011]
- Google Ads can import offline conversions, such as sales made by phone, so that phone-based sales are counted alongside online conversions in campaign results. [L1033.014]

Phone sale attribution procedure (Google Ads):

1. Store each customer's contact details in a CRM tool such as Klaviyo or Wicked Reports, so the customer is linked to their web activity when they call.
2. Look up the caller in the CRM to see their click history.
3. Confirm from the tagged links that the customer came from a Google ad.
4. Upload the offline sale with its click data in the offline conversions section of Google Ads so the sale is attributed to the campaign.

The process is cumbersome but gives Google the data it needs. [L0454.047]

Keyed and coupon-based response tracking:

- Advertisers without tracking often pay two to thirty-five times what their sales should cost, because they never see which ads produce the cheapest results. Keyed comparison can reveal cost gaps of several times between ads for the same product. [L1261.017]
- Changing ad size, pictures or type without a traced comparison often raises cost per reply while adding no extra sales. A bigger or more decorative ad must be proven to bring proportionally more sales. [L1261.018]
- Reply cost depends on how a coupon is redeemed. In one test, mailed sample requests cost about 70 cents each, while the same ads with coupons presented at a local store produced inquiries for 18 to 22 cents each. [L1261.145]

Store display test procedure:

1. Choose two comparable towns.
2. Run the window or store display in one town only.
3. Compare total product sales in both towns, not only the sales of the display store.
4. Keep the display spend only if total sales rise.

[L1261.151]

### Platform reporting quirks and delays

- TikTok's displayed spend in the ad manager is lower than the true spend and updates a minute or two late. A campaign showing $50 spent may have actually spent $60 to $80, so payment should be controlled manually. [L1382.044]
- Pinterest results take six to seven days to appear, slower than TikTok or Facebook. UTM parameters on the destination URL are the main way to track sales by campaign, and sales can appear with a delay because the platform records them slowly. [L1134.011]
- Pinterest's tracking delay is a platform recording issue, not a loss of sales. UTM parameters show the source of each sale. [L1135.046, L1134.011]

### Campaign-level UTM use for budget decisions

- Use UTM parameters to decide which ad set or campaign deserves more budget within a country, rather than relying on country-level data. [L1316.009]
- Sellers can compare campaigns across several accounts by reading UTM data, without linking the pixel to each account. [L0638.056]

## Validating and troubleshooting tracking

### Checking that pixels and tags fire

- Google Tag Assistant is a free tool for verifying that your call conversion tracking code is installed and works on your website. [L0234.014]
- To validate Google Analytics e-commerce tracking:
  1. Enable e-commerce tracking in Google Analytics.
  2. Let it run for 7 to 14 days, until about 20 orders are recorded.
  3. Open the transaction report (Conversions > E-commerce > Transactions).
  4. Compare the individual order numbers with the orders in your CMS.
  5. Confirm that they match. [L0575.025]
- If Google Ads shows a conversion tag as inactive, or reports 0% conversions while orders arrive, fix the tag through the feed app that set up the store's tags:
  1. Open the Google Shopping feed app in the Shopify App Store.
  2. Click Help inside that app.
  3. Send a screenshot of the Google Ads warning, such as "conversion tracking inactive".
  4. Ask the app to check that the tag sits in the correct place and is configured correctly, or to set it up again. [L0470.001]
- To confirm that a Google Ads conversion tag works, check whether conversions have been recorded since the tag was installed. The "no conversions" warning can appear simply because no sale has happened yet. If sales happen and the warning persists, contact the tracking app's support. [L1115.037]
- If the conversion tag can fire on a page that testers reach during setup, such as a thank-you or checkout page, Google Ads can record a conversion with no Shopify sale. Check which page triggers the conversion and correct the tag setup before trusting the conversion data. [L0960.077] *(Older advice, 2019.)*
- The Facebook Events Manager test view works as follows:
  - It shows incoming activity only while its page stays open in Events Manager during testing.
  - Test-mode results can differ from what live visitors generate.
  - Your personal ad preferences have no bearing on the test data. [L1027.022]
- Events sent by the Shopify partner integration show the setup method "Partner integration" in the test view. Their event IDs start with "sh-". Typical events include PageView, and View content with the status "Processed" after a product page is viewed. [L1027.023]
- The Meta pixel dataset ID and the ad account ID are both long numeric IDs of 16 digits. Check both on the Events Manager dataset page and in Ads Manager to confirm that the pixel is linked to the correct ad account. [L1027.024]
- To check the whole Facebook funnel:
  1. Open Facebook Events Manager and go through the funnel from page view to checkout.
  2. Confirm that each event fires at each step.
  3. If needed, check the store side with platform support.
  4. Create the custom metrics (hook rate, click-through rate, conversion rate) in Ads Manager.
  5. Find the weakest stage and fix it. [L0950.036]
- If audiences stay empty despite real website traffic, the pixel is usually installed incorrectly. Check every pixel's implementation before changing audiences or ads. [L1390.006] *(Older advice, 2021-22.)*
- Facebook does not always record every event. Content views and some add-to-cart counts can be undercounted. Treat these numbers as directional signals and judge sales against the store's own order records. [L1426.045]
- If the CPA column for a product stays empty, conversion tracking is probably not set up in the ad account. The per-product cost per conversion column, which CPA-based bidding needs, fills in only when tracking exists. Without it, the target CPA calculation has no current CPA to work from. [L1328.037]

### Duplicate and inflated counting

- If conversions show at double their real number in Google Ads, the purchase is probably tracked twice, for example with a Shopify app plus a manual Google code, or a Google Analytics goal plus an app. Keep one tracking method and disable or delete the other. [L0356.055] *(Older advice, 2019.)*
- The repeat rate in Google Ads conversion settings shows how many conversions are counted per conversion event. Interpret it as follows:

  | Repeat rate | Meaning | Source |
  |---|---|---|
  | Near 1 | Normal | [L0391.005] |
  | Slightly above 1 | Can happen when the pixel fires twice, for example after a page refresh on the confirmation page | [L0391.005] |
  | About 1.5 to 3 | Nearly every conversion is double counted; a serious tracking problem | [L0391.005, L0391.006] |

- A repeat rate well above 1 (roughly 1.5 to 3) means that nearly all purchases are double counted in Google Ads. This inflates conversions and ROAS. It is a known Shopify issue. Check whether the confirmation page fires the pixel more than once, and do not optimise campaigns on inflated numbers until the problem is resolved. [L0391.006]
- A campaign that appears to fail with a CBO scaling setup may have a correct setup. Verify the ad set's results with UTMs, because the ad manager can show more sales than were actually recorded. [L0349.021]

### Tracking gaps and acceptable variance

- Two sources give different thresholds for an acceptable gap. Apply them to different comparisons:

  | Comparison | Acceptable value | Source |
  |---|---|---|
  | Analytics or tracking against the invoicing system | Gap of up to about 5% is normal, because no single tool captures every route a customer takes to buy | [L0359.095] |
  | Facebook-tracked purchases against actual sales | Tracking rate of about 80 to 90%; 80% or more is acceptable | [L1158.040] |

- Sales not tracked by Facebook can be checked in Shopify Reports under Sessions by marketing, grouped by campaign, ad set and ad. [L1158.040]
- A gap of 50 or more daily Google Ads clicks against only 10 to 15 analytics visitors is a tracking fault. Fix analytics on the affected product pages before increasing ad spend. [L0934.079, L0934.121]
- Google Analytics usually records more sales than Google Ads for the same period, so the two platforms rarely show matching conversion numbers. [L0721.033]
- Google Ads and Google Analytics under-report conversions even when set up correctly. Causes include cookie blockers, privacy features, payment gateway redirects and draft orders. [L0256.007]
- Standard conversion tags under-track but do not over-track when installed correctly. Over-tracking points to a faulty installation or to multiple counting of one transaction. [L0721.019]
- Compare Facebook's purchase count in Ads Manager with real orders before deciding that a test has failed. Facebook can record fewer purchases than came through the store. [L1124.013]
- A conversion gap between Google Analytics and Google Ads has two possible paths:
  1. Compare purchase counts in Google Analytics and Google Ads for the same period.
  2. If purchases show in Analytics but not in Google Ads, check the attribution settings and increase the conversion window for the purchase conversion in Google Ads.
  3. If purchases show in neither, suspect a tagging or installation problem with the tracking code.
  
  Some gap is normal because of cross-device purchases and traffic from outside Google. [L1281.097] *(Older advice, 2022.)*
- Example: Google Ads showed 26 conversions and Google Analytics showed 53 for the same period. A gap of that size usually points to attribution settings, not to missing sales. [L1281.098] *(Older advice, 2022.)*
- Standard tracking relies on cookies, which are increasingly unreliable. iOS devices with restricted tracking, ad blockers and cookie blockers can stop conversion tags from firing. Apple's iOS 15 privacy changes limit email open tracking. Google has announced moving away from cookie-based tracking, but no replacement was available at the time of the lesson. [L0721.018] *(Older advice, 2021-22.)*
- Reconcile Google Analytics, Shopify Analytics and individual app dashboards before drawing conclusions. They often show different values for the same metric, and the data is easy to misread. [L0390.013]

### Shopify vs ad platform order mismatches

- To correct for under-tracked conversions:
  1. Compare the total orders in Shopify with the conversions tracked in the ad platform for the same period.
  2. Calculate the number of untracked orders.
  3. Check the Source/Medium report in GA to see what share of traffic each source contributes.
  4. Allocate the untracked orders and their revenue to the sources in proportion to their traffic share. Example: if 90% of traffic is Google Ads, assign 90% of 15 untracked orders to Google Ads.
  5. Recalculate cost per transaction and ROAS with the adjusted figures. [L0721.020] *(Older advice, 2021-22.)*
- Example discrepancy: Shopify shows 57 orders in a month and Google Ads tracks 40. If 17 of the untracked orders were draft orders, the difference is explained. Remaining untracked orders are typically caused by cookie or browser limits. [L0721.017] *(Older advice, 2021-22.)*
- Example of a large gap: one high-ticket dropshipping store, November 1-15, 2020. Google Analytics reported $68,000 revenue from 21 transactions. Google Ads reported 14.26 conversions. Actual Shopify revenue was $79,000. The gap came from draft orders and payment gateway redirects that broke the tracking chain. [L0256.008] *(Older advice, 2021-22.)*
- A conversion without a matching Shopify order can be a customer who paid a PayPal invoice sent manually instead of through checkout. Check PayPal for the payment before concluding that tracking is broken. [L0960.078] *(Older advice, 2019.)*
- After PayPal Express buttons are removed, the abandoned cart count can rise, because those shoppers were previously untracked. This is expected and does not signal a new problem. Judge the change by completed orders and conversion rate, not by abandoned cart numbers alone. [L0575.033]

### Draft and phone orders

- Draft orders created in Shopify, for phone sales, discounts or custom orders with extra shipping, are not tracked by Google Ads or GA conversion tags. Shopify's draft order checkout cannot have tracking codes installed. A store can therefore show more Shopify orders than the ad platforms report. [L0721.016] *(Older advice, 2021-22.)*
- Track draft order revenue separately each month, outside your tracked conversion data. Then you know how much additional revenue to reallocate to your traffic sources when calculating actual return on ad spend, based on each source's share of new users. [L0256.017] *(Older advice, 2021-22.)*

### Cookie blockers and payment redirects

- Visitors with a cookie blocker, or a browser that does not support cookies, do not have their conversion recorded in Google Analytics or Google Ads. [L0256.003] *(Older advice, 2021-22.)*
- Payment providers such as Bread, Klarna and Zip Money redirect buyers away from your site during checkout and return them to your thank-you page afterwards. Google cannot link the final visit to earlier visits. The sale is therefore attributed to the payment provider as referral traffic, not to your original traffic source. [L0256.004] *(Older advice, 2021-22.)*

### Bot traffic

- Sessions from an unknown location that cannot be linked to any channel in the Shopify sessions report may be bot traffic. These sessions can often be ignored when they do not lead to add-to-carts or purchases. [L0363.082]
- Bot or internal traffic that spikes right after an ad launch distorts conversion rates. Publish ads about six to seven hours before the planned start, so that launch traffic can be identified and excluded from funnel calculations. [L1259.003]
- If a launch spike of bot traffic cannot be separated cleanly, estimate it by subtracting the normal daily visitor count from the spike day. Use the cleaned visitor number when calculating funnel rates. [L1260.071]
- Traffic spikes that come from a single cloud server provider, often with one product page viewed per visit, are usually automated crawlers from platforms such as Shopify, Facebook or Google. They are not real customers, so do not react to them as sales opportunities. [L1088.064] *(Older advice, 2020.)*

### Realtime tests

- To test GA4 Realtime:
  1. Open the GA4 property, not the Universal Analytics property.
  2. Open the Realtime report.
  3. Visit a random product page on your store.
  4. Confirm that your visit appears with the page and location on the map. If it does not, recheck the GA4 setup from the GA4 lesson. [L0835.086]
- To use Google Analytics real-time reports:
  1. Open the real-time report several times a day to confirm that traffic has not dropped.
  2. Check where visitors come from and which pages they are on.
  3. Use conversion tracking and reports to see which pages and products make the most money.
  4. Review pages with high bounce rates, where visitors leave almost immediately, and fix them. [L1088.063] *(Older advice, 2020.)*

### Test orders

- To test purchase tracking with a $1 product:
  1. Create a temporary product priced at $1 in the store.
  2. Buy it as a test purchase.
  3. Check that the order appears in Shopify and in analytics, and that the test does not need to be fulfilled before Google sees it.
  4. Delete the temporary product afterwards. [L1287.064]
- A test order placed only to measure how many orders a store receives can be refunded afterwards, according to the method described, so the measurement cost stays limited. Confirm the platform's refund rules for such orders before relying on this. [L1219.057]
- Dummy test orders estimate sales but are not fully reliable. The lesson suggests they work about nine times in ten. Treat one dummy order result as a hypothesis, and confirm it with a second order or a second source. [L1385.007]

### Diagnosing traffic and conversion anomalies

#### Normal variation and first checks

- Small daily fluctuations in traffic and sales are normal even when nothing is broken, especially with low traffic volumes. Use date-range analysis in Google Analytics to detect patterns over weeks rather than reacting to daily variations. [L0243.041] *(Older advice, 2019.)*
- To analyse a traffic decline in Google Analytics by source:
  1. Log into the Google Analytics account.
  2. Navigate to the left column menu > Traffic Sources.
  3. Select a specific date range, for example March 1 through April 3.
  4. Uncheck "All Sources".
  5. Check one source, for example Google, to view the traffic graph for that source only.
  6. Repeat for each traffic source (Facebook, Instagram, Pinterest, etc.) to identify which source lost traffic.
  7. Investigate specific source changes separately. [L0243.010] *(Older advice, 2019.)*
- When traffic jumps sharply, first check whether the jump is spread across all campaigns or limited to one section. Then break the data down by day to see whether it was a short spike or a sustained increase. [L0853.104] *(Older advice, 2021.)*
- To find the cause of a traffic spike you want to repeat:
  1. Check whether the spike happened across all campaigns or in one section.
  2. Break the data down by day and see whether it was a short peak or steady growth.
  3. Check whether the spike was a tracking glitch or a real change.
  4. Identify what started it and whether it lasted, then repeat the conditions that worked. [L0853.105] *(Older advice, 2021.)*
- If the conversion rate looks abnormally high or low, check that sales tracking is set up correctly before optimising. Support from the Google Shopping feed app can verify the configuration. [L1092.079] *(Older advice, 2022.)*

#### Conversion rate

- Conversion rate = completed orders ÷ visitors. Example: 1 sale from 2,444 visits gives 0.04%. If half of those visits were bot traffic, the same sale gives 0.08% on the real visitors. [L0626.011]
- Before judging a store's conversion rate, check in Shopify analytics where the visits come from and how many are bot traffic, organic or paid. Bot traffic inflates the visit count and makes the conversion rate look lower than it really is. [L0626.012]

#### Sudden drops in ad traffic

- To diagnose a sudden traffic drop in Google Ads:
  1. Open Google Ads and go to Change history.
  2. Choose All campaigns.
  3. Set the date range from the last normal traffic period until today.
  4. Compare the performance graph with the logged changes.
  5. If a change lines up with the drop, click Undo on it. [L0934.123]
- A sudden drop in Google Ads traffic with no logged account change can come from a Merchant Center product feed issue, such as products that are no longer approved. Check Merchant Center for errors and warnings before assuming seasonality. [L0934.120]
- To review sudden drops in daily traffic:
  1. Open Google Analytics and review the daily traffic since the start of the month.
  2. Look for sharp drops and note the date.
  3. Check whether Google Ads spend stopped on that date.
  4. Check whether a product was suspended on that date.
  5. Reverse-engineer the cause from those records. [L1111.153] *(Older advice, 2020.)*
- Troubleshoot a sudden drop in ad performance in this order:
  1. Confirm that the store works functionally: checkout, page loading, product images and shipping coverage.
  2. Check what changed between the working and failing periods.
  3. Test ad creatives by process of elimination. Cut the newest ad set or creative for a few days to see whether the old ones recover. [L0950.026]
- Audit your ad accounts after a seasonal dip in sales, for example after the November to December peak, to confirm that tracking and campaign settings still work as expected. [L0665.101] *(Older advice, 2022.)*

#### Zero sales and declining bestsellers

- To investigate a zero-sales month:
  1. Compare Google Analytics traffic for the zero-sales month with earlier months to see whether traffic dropped.
  2. Identify which bestselling products lost traffic and which source stopped sending it to them.
  3. Confirm that your ads still get impressions and clicks.
  4. Search your bestsellers on Google Shopping and check whether competitors started undercutting prices or using other tactics. [L1279.073] *(Older advice, 2021.)*
- When an ad campaign shows zero add-to-carts, also check the store's own analytics, for example Shopify reports. If the store analytics also show zero, the pixel alone is probably not the cause. Treat the product, offer or landing experience as the likely problem. [L0361.008]

#### Sales pace and paid traffic

- PPAds shows live sales for a store's products, but per the lesson it shows sales only while the store is open and active in the tool. It does not show sales from a week ago or lifetime totals, so it gives a partial, current view. Use it as one data point, not as a complete sales history. [L1385.006]
- Sales that arrive in short bursts with long gaps can suggest paid traffic running in the background. Compare the sales pace with the view pace. If views are high but sales are small and irregular, look for paid promotion before trusting the organic numbers. [L1385.008]

#### Video traffic and shadowban check

- To check for a shadowban in the video analysis:
  1. Wait a few days after posting, because the video analysis takes time to appear.
  2. Open the video analysis of the video.
  3. Scroll down to the Traffic source section.
  4. Compare the percentage from "For You" with the percentage from "Personal profile".
  5. If Personal profile is under about 3% and most views come from For You, the account is probably not restricted and the video was simply weak.
  6. If the share from followers is higher than the share from For You, the video is only shown to followers and is unlikely to go viral. [L1381.006]

#### Abandoned carts and feed changes

- After PayPal Express buttons are removed, judge the change by completed orders and conversion rate, not by abandoned cart numbers. [L0575.033]

### Offline conversion uploads

- When pixel purchase data is missing, for example after an iOS update:
  1. Export all purchases from the store as a CSV file.
  2. Upload the purchase file to Facebook, so the platform learns which purchases happened on the store.
  3. Wait about 1 to 2 hours, then check the actual ROAS in Ads Manager.
  4. If tracking is still missing after a longer period, contact Facebook support and ask for the data to be restored. [L0645.035] *(Older advice, 2021-22.)*
- To upload offline conversions for consistent sales:
  1. Export all orders as a CSV file from the store.
  2. Open Meta Business Manager and find the offline conversions or offline upload option.
  3. Import the CSV file.
  4. Wait about 30 minutes for the conversions to appear in the Facebook dashboard. [L1345.095]
- Manual CSV offline conversion upload is a practical fallback when sales are steady, for example two to five orders a day, and no automated tracking tool is in place. [L1345.096]

## Daily and weekly ad analysis workflow

### Reporting layers: snapshot and funnel

- Split the ad view into two column categories. Snapshot metrics show current performance at one point in time. Funnel metrics show how performance changes as viewers move from awareness to action. [L0008.002, L0640.001]
- Snapshot columns: ad set name, last significant edit, daily budget, amount spent, result for the chosen objective, cost per result. [L0640.001]
- Funnel columns: impressions, CPM, 3-second video plays, hook rate, hold rate, unique CTR, unique clicks, cost per click, adds to cart, purchases, conversion rate, ROAS. Together they show where the funnel wins or loses. [L0640.001]
- Ad analysis builds a picture from inference, not from hard rules. Combine several metrics and reason from the evidence instead of judging by one number. Low spend and early data make single metrics especially unreliable. [L0640.006]
- ROAS is not a core north-star metric for ad analysis on its own. It summarises return across all user actions and is a major driver of overall profitability. [L0640.013]
- Purchases and ROAS matter most. Compare ROAS with break-even ROAS: above break-even is profitable, below is not. Soft metrics (hook rate, hold rate, CTR) are improved only when bottom-of-funnel sales are already profitable, or when weak sales need their cause diagnosed. [L0652.010]

### Ads Manager column setup

**Customise the column set**

1. In Ads Manager, open the column menu, scroll down and choose Customize columns. Click Customize columns to add or remove metrics. [L1024.024, L1024.045]
2. Type the metric name into the search field. If it does not appear, search again with the exact metric name. [L1024.045]
3. Drag the chosen metrics into funnel order. [L1024.045]
4. Save the layout as a named preset, named for the store, then click Apply. [L1024.024, L1024.045]
5. Optional extras: engagement rate ranking, quality ranking, and conversion rate ranking (the last is relevant when the purchase objective is used). [L1024.025]

**Column order in the reporting view (top of funnel to bottom)**

1. Campaign, Delivery, Asset name, Budget. [L1024.025]
2. CPM. [L1024.025]
3. Impressions. [L1024.025]
4. Reach. [L1024.025]
5. Hook rate (custom metric). [L1024.025]
6. Hold rate (custom metric). [L1024.025]
7. Link clicks. [L1024.025]
8. CTR (link click-through rate), placed after Hold rate. [L1024.025]
9. CPC (cost per link click). [L1024.025]
10. Total content views and Cost per content view. [L1024.025]
11. Total add to cart and Cost per add to cart. [L1024.025]
12. Total initiated checkout and Cost per initiated checkout. [L1024.025]
13. Total purchases and Cost per purchase. [L1024.025]
14. Purchase conversion value. [L1024.025]
15. Amount spent. [L1024.025]
16. Total purchase ROAS. [L1024.025]

- Columns run from top of funnel to bottom: first ad delivery metrics, then on-site metrics, then checkout and purchase metrics. This order shows where prospects drop out. [L1024.026]
- Track the higher-intent on-site actions (add to cart, initiate checkout, purchases), each with its total and cost per action. These show where buyers drop out between first click and purchase. [L1024.040]

**Custom metrics**

- Hook rate: click Create custom metric, name it Hook rate, select format Percentage, use the formula 3-second video plays / impressions. Click Create metric and drag it to the desired column position. [L1024.029]
- Video Attention (same formula, other name): Columns > Customize columns, click Create custom metric, name it Video Attention, set the format to percentage, and define it as 3-second video plays divided by impressions. [L0644.002]
- Hold rate: click Create custom metric, name it Hold rate, select format Percentage, use the formula Through-plays / impressions. Place it next to Hook rate. [L1024.031]

**Columns for reading a test (phone view)**

- Results set to "Website purchases", Cost per result, Amount spent, Budget (shown as "Using ad set budget" or "Daily"), Impressions and CPM (cost per 1,000 impressions), under the "Performance and clicks" column group. [L1124.012]
- Columns used for reading a test on a phone view, in the lesson's order: CPC (cost per link click), Link clicks, Adds to cart, Checkouts, Purchases, Cost per add to cart, ROAS (return on ad spend), Frequency, Delivery. [L0348.033]

**Required metrics per Meta video ad**

| Metric | Definition |
|---|---|
| Ad name | Identifier |
| Amount spent | Total spend on the ad |
| Amount spent % | Share of total campaign spend |
| Budget | Budget allocated to the ad or ad set |
| Current delivery | Active, Learning, Learning Limited or Off |
| Start date | When the ad began running |
| Impressions | Total impressions |
| Reach | Unique people reached |
| CPM | Cost per 1,000 impressions |
| Link clicks | Total link clicks |
| Cost per unique outbound click | Cost per unique click off-platform |
| Landing page views | Total landing page views |
| Cost per landing page view | Cost per LPV |
| Add to carts | Total add-to-cart events |
| Cost per add to cart | Cost per ATC |
| Checkouts initiated | Total checkout events |
| Cost per checkout | Cost per checkout initiated |
| Purchases | Total purchases |
| Cost per purchase | CPA |
| Purchase value | Total revenue for ROAS |
| ROAS | Return on ad spend |
| Video plays at 25%, 50%, 75%, 100% | Counts of video views at each mark |
| Page | Facebook or Instagram page running the ad |
| Destination link | Landing page URL |

Optional but useful: frequency, CTR, 3-second video views, ThruPlays, and breakdowns by age, gender and placement (Feed, Stories, Reels). [L0613.047]

**Visual flags**

- Format the ROAS column with conditional colours (green, yellow or red) by ROAS value. This shows at a glance which campaigns are profitable against break-even. [L1476.042]
- Conditional formatting for ROAS targets: add a column for the ROAS target, highlight rows that beat it (raise bids) and rows under the 2.75 threshold (clear dead weight or lower bids). Revenue target cells can turn red when missed and green when hit. [L1236.011]
- Cost per view: with a KPI of 0.05 USD (5 cents) per video view, format cells green at or below 0.05 and yellow at or above 0.05 as a warning. In the example, a cost per view around 3 cents was a good result, and cells above the threshold were flagged for review. [L1236.015]

**Auction data**

- Ads Manager shows auction overlap, audience saturation and auction competition for an audience. These show how much your own ad sets compete for the same people. [L0648.050]

### Daily snapshot check

- Review profit and loss and ad performance first thing each morning, before working through the task list. Knowing spend and profit before creative work starts keeps daily decisions tied to real numbers. [L1181.026]
- Keep a reference document with ad metric guidance and annotated example images for the regular ad review. Turn any review action from that document directly into a task so it is not lost. [L1181.029]
- Each day, review cost per add to cart, CTR, CPC, CPM and the engagement rate ranking for each ad. Cut losers early when soft metrics stay weak. Keep an ad running only if its signals improve from day to day. [L1195.049]
- Amount spent is the strongest signal of where the platform sees opportunity, so read it before judging. Do not force budget into ads the algorithm is not funding; diagnose why they are not getting spend. Efficiency without scale is a vanity result (for example 10x ROAS on $5 of spend), so look for ads that are both efficient and funded. [L0613.045, L0613.062]
- Meta video KPI hierarchy, in priority order:

| Tier | Metrics | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| 1 Spend signals | Amount spent; amount spent %; budget vs spend; delivery status (Active, Learning, Learning Limited, Inactive); start date | Read the algorithm's allocation |
| 2 Efficiency | ROAS; cost per purchase | Profitability |
| 3 Funnel diagnostics | CPM; cost per unique outbound click; cost per landing page view; cost per add to cart; cost per checkout; conversion rates between stages | Locate where the funnel breaks |
| 4 Creative performance | Video plays at 25%, 50%, 75%, 100%; hook rate, hold rate and drop-off | Attention and engagement |
| 5 Context | Page running the ad; destination link | Background |

[L0613.046]

- Build a daily dashboard that combines ad metrics with product margin to show when to scale a winning ad or test new creatives. Mentors can use the same view to find where a brand's bottleneck sits. [L1225.066]

### Metrics and targets

- ROAS = Revenue / Ad spend. Example: 14,580 / 456 = 31.97, while the report showed a ROAS of 31. Confirm your sheet uses the same definition and rounding before setting targets. A ROAS target of 20x means revenue should be 20 times the ad spend. [L1236.022]
- Blended ROAS is the right level for judging a campaign, together with product profitability. Low ROAS on a product with few clicks is a sample-size problem, not a verdict. [L0969.052]
- Track ROAS and revenue against separate targets, because each can be missed independently. If ROAS is above target but revenue is below, the account is efficient but lacks volume: bid more aggressively and accept lower ROAS to gain sales. If ROAS falls, find the cause by checking which campaigns or ad sets lost efficiency. [L1236.008]
- Compare each campaign's actual cost per sale, ROAS and cost per click with the calculated target values before changing anything. If the campaign performs below target, revise the bid, the price or the conversion rate. If it has room below target, you may be too cautious and can bid more aggressively. [L0686.019]
- A campaign can be above breakeven and still have room to improve. If CPA is too high relative to the target, work on the funnel: ad creative, average order value and landing page. [L0445.010]
- When CAC looks poor, the ad with the highest spend is still the main signal of what works, and it should guide the next step. Do not judge the whole account by one weak metric. [L1038.013]
- For an awareness campaign without conversion tracking yet, judge performance on video views and cost per view instead of ROAS or revenue, which cannot be measured yet. Set a cost-per-view KPI, such as 0.05 USD, to check whether the cost of views stays in line. [L1236.023]

### Analysis workflow

**Ad analysis workflow, in order**

1. Review snapshot metrics first (daily budget, total spend, results, cost per result) to see if the ad is profitable. [L0067.038]
2. Use funnel metrics to pinpoint where the problem is (attention, interest, desire or action stage). [L0067.038]
3. Use the ADA framework (named AIDA in L0640.007): check hook rate (attention), CTR (interest), conversion rate (desire), ROAS (action). [L0067.038, L0640.007]
4. If a weak area is identified, do a creative review against the checklist. [L0067.038]
5. Form a hypothesis about improvement. [L0067.038]
6. Create a test ad with a single change, keeping the control. [L0067.038]
7. Run it for 3 days minimum. [L0067.038]
8. Evaluate lift. [L0067.038]

**Protocol for comparing ad sets**

1. Compare interests against each other using funnel metrics, starting at the top of the funnel (hook rate / attention). [L0640.015]
2. Move down to interest (CTR), then desire (add to cart, conversion rate) and action (ROAS). [L0640.015]
3. Note where each ad set falls behind and focus improvement effort on the weakest stage. [L0640.015]

**Meta video analysis, in order**

1. Spend distribution: rank ads by amount spent, compute each share of total, utilisation against budget, spend per day and delivery status. Measure how concentrated spend is in the top 3 ads. Check whether new ads are ramping or starved. [L0613.048]
2. Delivery status: count ads by status, the share of spend on Active ads, and why Learning Limited ads are stuck (audience too small, budget too low, or creative underperforming). [L0613.048]
3. Ad age: bucket ads and compare performance by bucket. [L0613.048]
4. Efficiency: compare ROAS and CPA against targets and segment them. [L0613.048]
5. Funnel: build the cost cascade from CPM to cost per purchase and the stage conversion rates, and find the weakest stage per ad. [L0613.048]
6. Landing pages: group performance by destination link to see whether a weak page drags down good creative. [L0613.048]
7. Creative: compute hook rate, retention between marks and completion; find drop-off points. [L0613.048]
8. Identify the one primary constraint. [L0613.048]
9. Give next-best-dollar and next-best-week recommendations. [L0613.048]
10. Write the executive summary and an ad-by-ad scorecard. [L0613.048]

**Required inputs before analysis**

1. Brand name, product, price points, average order value, mechanism, primary audience, problem solved. [L0613.061]
2. Campaign objective, bid strategy, cost or bid cap value, optimisation event, total budget and start date. [L0613.061]
3. Target ROAS, target CPA, other efficiency targets, break-even ROAS and the current priority (scale, efficiency or both). [L0613.061]
4. For each ad: hook in the first 3 seconds, main message or angle, format, talent, offer and CTA, and video length. [L0613.061]
5. The metrics table for each ad. [L0613.061]

**Final deliverable order**

1. Brand and campaign summary. [L0613.060]
2. Spend distribution. [L0613.060]
3. Efficiency. [L0613.060]
4. Funnel diagnostic. [L0613.060]
5. Creative performance. [L0613.060]
6. Primary constraint. [L0613.060]
7. Next-best-dollar. [L0613.060]
8. Next-best-week. [L0613.060]
9. One-page executive summary covering performance snapshot against target spend, ROAS and CPA, primary constraint, next-best-dollar with ads and amounts, top 3 priorities, and a 30-day outlook. [L0613.060]
10. Ad-by-ad scorecards. Each shows a score out of 100 split into spend signal, efficiency, funnel health and creative performance, a 2-3 sentence diagnosis, a recommendation of SCALE, OPTIMIZE, MONITOR or KILL, and specific actions. [L0613.060]

### Reading the funnel

**Stages**

- Read the funnel in three stages: attention (hook rate on the first three seconds), interest (click-through rate), and desire (conversion rate). A weak stage points to where the funnel breaks down. [L0950.017]
- Use the AIDA framework to map the funnel: Attention = hook rate, Interest = click-through rate, Desire and Action = conversion rate (add to cart and purchase), and overall result = ROAS. Check the stages in order to find the weakest point. [L0640.007]
- A high hook rate only shows that viewers pay attention. It does not prove that the traffic will buy. A lower hook rate can still convert well if the audience is the right one, so always read hook rate together with CTR and conversion rate. [L0950.080]
- Judge a product first by hook rate (look rate) and CTR on ads. If both are low, change the creatives or accept that the product does not attract interest. [L1108.021]
- Ad performance is judged first by CTR, CPM and CPC. Only if these look healthy should the store and funnel be analysed for weak points. [L1131.015]

**Metric combinations**

- Strong attention + interest + desire = strong ROAS. [L0258.022]
- Strong attention + interest + weak desire = normal ROAS. Suspect an ad-landing-page mismatch, technical problems, high shipping costs, low trust or high price. [L0258.022]
- Weak attention + good interest + good desire = test new hooks, because the problem is not painful enough. [L0258.022]
- Strong attention + weak interest + good desire = test messaging in the ad middle section or refine targeting. [L0258.022]
- CTR and CPC good, but people rarely add to cart or conversion rate is very low: the ad is working, and the store or landing page is the problem. The fix is store messaging, offer or a more educational page, not a new ad. [L1131.016]
- Hook rate and CTR good but sales missing: the problem is usually the store. Keep working on the store before dropping the product. [L1108.022]
- CTR and CPC look good but sales are missing: the problem is usually the store, not the ad. Fix the website and offer first, then judge the product by ROAS. [L1173.037]
- Strong CTR but no sales: the offer is usually the problem and should be changed before the ads are changed. [L1469.003]
- On TikTok, a CPM of about $5 to $7 is acceptable. If CPM is fine but CTR is low, the problem sits at the creative level. Use CPM, CPC and CTR together to find which part of the funnel fails. [L1377.020]

**Creative and iteration**

- When iterating ads: if an ad runs acceptably with one or two improvable elements, replace only those specific elements. If it performs poorly overall, test a completely new concept. [L0258.023]
- In video retention, a large drop before the 25% mark points to a hook problem. A large drop between 25% and 50% points to weak middle content. A large drop between 50% and 75% points to a problem in the pitch or offer. Strong retention through 75% combined with low completion points to a weak call to action. [L0613.069]

**Sessions and sales**
